2026-09-16 | Claude | Conference deadline is 2026-10-31 (D-45). Wrote docs/학술대회_일정역산_2026-09-16.md. Three blockers identified and mitigated: (1) anthesis-to-harvest is ~45-60 days so no harvest event is observable in the window unless already-fruiting plants are bought immediately - this is the critical path; (2) harvest events would be too few for a regression target, so the RNN task changes to next-timestep fruit state prediction, where every session pair is a free label and harvest date becomes a derived result validated on the few observed events; (3) Stage 3 leaf instance segmentation will not be reliable in time, so the conference scope narrows to fruits only - leaves, node graph and pruning suggestions move to future work. Schedule has capture ending 10/12 with code built in parallel, and two RNN-independent results (rig reconstruction accuracy, 3D vs 2D fruit traits) structured as standalone chapters so the talk survives an RNN that underperforms | docs/학술대회_일정역산_2026-09-16.md

Also added a capture-time budget: 12 plants at 36 views is about 3.2 h per capture day and 35 h cumulative, so the schedule now offers two mitigations - cut view count from the pilot sweep, or split into a 6-plant 3D group and a 6-plant two-view-only group, which preserves the 3D-vs-2D comparison within the 3D group while keeping all 12 sequences for the RNN | docs/학술대회_RNN_설계메모_2026-09-16.md, docs/학술대회_일정역산_2026-09-16.md

2026-09-16 | Claude | Environment logging fixed at three manual readings per day (lights-on, mid-photoperiod, lights-off), with the times pinned to what each reading represents. Recorded that this is sufficient: GDD's standard form needs only daily max and min, DIF uses reading 3 paired with the next day's reading 1 to bound the dark period, and DLI needs no daily reading at all since LED PPFD is constant during the photoperiod - one position-specific PPFD measurement plus the schedule gives it exactly. Per-item frequency separated so not every item is measured three times: temp/RH three times, EC/pH/water level once at lights-on, PPFD only on change. Recommended a cheap USB temp/RH logger, since 135 manual readings over 45 days will have gaps and temperature cannot be recorded retroactively | docs/학술대회_RNN_설계메모_2026-09-16.md, docs/촬영당일_체크리스트_2026-09-16.md

2026-09-17 | Claude Code | Implemented the leaf light-use pipeline on the user's explicit instruction ("implement the basic flow and the environment variables"), which overrides the brief's design-review-first request; the brief's schedule/acceptance-criteria deliverables were NOT produced. New package tomato3d/light/ (units, frames, scene, geometry, raytrace, budget, validate, compare, pipeline) and CLI tomato3d/cli/run_light.py. Flow: light_scene.json (every lamp/placement/optics value carries value+status+source; unavailable is null and propagates, never 0) + leaf_surfaces.csv (triangles, board frame) and/or leaf_patches.csv (centroid/area/normal tilt+azimuth rectangles) grouped by geometry_variant_id + optional ppfd_readings.csv (grow frame, calibration/evaluation split) -> direct PPFD per leaf triangle with shading (cos^m lamp normalised to PPF, opaque non-leaf surfaces, leaf transmittance once per leaf crossed) -> leaf light map, per-leaf/plant/scenario light, DLI, absorbed photons, kWh/day, absorbed umol/J -> sensor predictions, one lamp calibration factor per scenario fitted on calibration readings against the no_plants variant and applied to all variants, evaluation MAE/RMSE/bias -> scenario comparison vs as_built and geometry-variant comparison vs a reference variant (research questions 1 and 2). Schemas and CSV contracts added to tomato3d/io/schemas.py (LightScene and friends, 3 input + 7 output tables, LightRunSummary); tomato3d/io/tables.py gains read_table_columns; schemas gains public format_maybe_float; Settings gains light_scene_path (config dir, not in CONFIG_FILE_PROPERTIES because the file does not exist yet) and light_runs_dir (outputs/light). Tests: tests/test_light_raytrace.py, test_light_inputs.py, test_light_pipeline.py, conftest light helpers, example inputs in tests/fixtures/light/ (all values placeholders, readings synthetic). 183 passed (116 before + 67). Mutation-checked six defects (per-triangle leaf counting, missing (m+1) normalisation, missing mm2->m2, calibrating on evaluation readings, forgetting absorptance, linear dimmed power): each fails a test. NOT done / needs others: (1) config/light_scene.json does not exist - the human creates it from tests/fixtures/light/light_scene.example.json after measuring lamps; I did not write config/. (2) CONVENTIONS.md should record the third frame (grow frame, mm, +Z up, origin described in the scene; board->grow only in tomato3d/light/frames.py::board_to_grow_mm) - proposal only, not edited. (3) Stage 2/3 reconstruction is still stubbed; leaf_surfaces.csv is the table it should write. Codex T6 (view-count sweep) is the natural producer of per-subset geometry variants; I did not implement view-subset selection to avoid duplicating T6. (4) The referenced session cse_01AjdpW4NnEftAXbs8KfLQUx could not be found from this environment; the non-competitive repo light-sim/ and alocasia-farm placement code were read as reference only (differences: triangle leaf surfaces instead of cylinder canopies with Beer-Lambert LAI; cos^m emission with cutoff instead of a Lambertian cone; no placement optimiser). Assumptions: plant placement = pose of the capture board lying flat under the pot on the shelf (yaw + translation; z = pot base height); a ray crossing a leaf is attenuated once per (plant, organ, leaflet) surface, which also prevents double counting at shared triangle edges but under-counts a genuinely rolled leaf crossed twice; transmitted light keeps its direction; reflection is not traced so predictions are a lower bound where reflection matters; readings supported only at full output; dimmed-segment power is never scaled linearly (unavailable unless measured); leaf area counted once for two-sided leaves; one optics set for all leaves. Unclear in the brief: numeric acceptance criteria for PPFD error, which readings are calibration vs evaluation, whether leaflets need separate identities, and how the plant pose under the LEDs is to be measured - all left as inputs, none guessed | tomato3d/light/, tomato3d/cli/run_light.py, tomato3d/io/schemas.py, tomato3d/io/tables.py, tomato3d/config.py, tests/test_light_*.py, tests/conftest.py, tests/fixtures/light/, README.md

2026-09-17 | Claude Code | Reviewed Codex's tools/leaf_light/ + tools/light_study.py (16:01-16:40). No Claude-owned file changed; it reuses tomato3d.light (load_geometry, direct_light, leaf_receivers, calibration_factor) instead of duplicating it. Verified: tests/ 183 passed; tools/tests 30 passed ONLY with --basetemp under build/. Findings for Codex, most important first, not fixed by Claude Code: (1) optics.run requires exactly one plant per scene, but 12 plants share the growing space; unless evaluation readings are taken with the evaluated plant alone under the lamps (neighbours removed, as calibration requires the empty space), neighbour shade is unmodelled and is scored as reconstruction error. The protocol/templates do not say this. (2) Reconstruction outputs leaf triangles only; the 5x5x7 cm pot, stem and neighbouring pots are absent occluders. Sensor sites below the pot rim or beside the stem are biased; restrict evaluation sites to above the rim, or add the pot as organ_class=pot triangles (tomato3d.light already treats non-leaf classes as opaque). (3) `python -m pytest tools/tests` gives 3 failed + 17 errors because common.output_dir refuses tmp_path outside build/; the suite needs a tools/tests/conftest.py that sets basetemp under build/, or the command documented. (4) By design every observed run is currently status=failed (blockers physical_print_not_verified, scale_reference_not_measured): the human must verify the print and measure the scale block in config/board_spec.json, then re-run prepare; captures taken before that remain usable. (5) Codex LOG entry says 'Started' with no completion entry, and the frozen defaults (8 evaluation plants x 3 visits x 9 sites x 3 replicates = 648 in-canopy readings, NRMSE <= 20 %, non-inferiority 5 pp, smartphone capture) are labelled approved; Claude Code cannot see that approval. Pot height (user question): pot base sits directly on the board, so reconstructed leaf z already includes the pot; placement z = grow-frame height of the pot base when growing (tray/mat/reservoir floor), minus any spacer thickness used under the pot during capture; never add the 70 mm pot height again | tools/leaf_light/, tools/tests/, handoff/LOG.md

2026-09-17 | Claude Code | Built a leaf point annotator at the user's request (no owner column covers a labeling UI; placed under build/ as a generated artifact, not in tools/). build/leaf_point_annotator/leaf_point_annotator.html, published as claude.ai artifact https://claude.ai/artifact/HEKuFH4sbesRBmne8e79aS (capabilities db + downloads, private). Loads photos locally (never uploaded) plus capture.json from tools/leaf_light prepare, or Stage 1 poses.csv + intrinsics JSON; marks per leaflet boundary points (b0..bN-1, auto-numbered), interior points (i0..) and contours; draws epipolar curves from the ChArUco poses (OpenCV k1-k6/p1-p2 distortion) and a triangulated predicted position; flags points in <2 photos, reprojection error > 2 px, parallax < 2 deg, and boundary points more than 3 px outside the same image's contour. Exports exactly tools/leaf_light POINT_COLUMNS/CONTOUR_COLUMNS (+ worklog row per variant); a selected capture variant restricts photos and keeps annotations separate (independent-subset annotation for question 2). Touch: tap marks, long-press selects, drag moves/pans, pinch zooms; narrow screens show one photo at a time; phone photos with camera names can be mapped to frames by sorted order after confirmation. Server store: poses/<session_id> (compact capture) and annotations/<session_id>__<variant_id> (leaflets, operator, seconds); Claude can read these with ArtifactData and write points.csv/contours.csv into the project. Verified with build/leaf_point_annotator/make_testdata.py (41 synthetic frames, distorted camera, curved leaflet): JS projection vs cv2.projectPoints max 4e-13 px, undistort round trip 2e-13 px, triangulation 7e-14 mm, epipolar curve within 1.1e-4 px of the true point; simulated pointer taps land within 1e-13 px; exported CSV -> tools.leaf_light.reconstruction.reconstruct -> v41_s00 leaflet reconstructed, 6 triangles, max vertex error 0.0004 mm. NOT verified: db save and downloads inside the live claude.ai viewer. Finding for Codex (tools/leaf_light/reconstruction.py, not changed): the per-triangle contour test rejects every triangle when a hand-drawn contour passes more than contour_tolerance_px (3 px) inside a clicked boundary point, so a contour that cuts a leaf tip silently removes the whole leaflet (reason no_contour_supported_triangles); reproduced with a corner-cutting contour. The annotator now warns about it; consider testing only triangle interiors or enlarging the contour tolerance | build/leaf_point_annotator/, handoff/LOG.md

2026-10-08 | Claude | Read four user-supplied papers and applied the one actionable finding. Barbosa et al. 2019 (Plant Cell Environ 42:1600): normalised perimeter (perimeter^2/area) is genotype-dependent and NOT influenced by irradiance, EFD shape differences between light treatments small, while SLA and gas exchange did move -> FSP v1 assumption recorded: light drives area and dry mass, blade outline shape parameters fixed across light; the real normalised-perimeter target (p50 2.63) is a material constant, supporting the Addendum G pool bias as a sampling error. Marti et al. 2006 (J Exp Bot 57:2037) used as the diagnostic basis for genotype-by-observation (sp determinate; d BR-biosynthesis co-segregating with dark-green rugose leaves; GA3 alone weak, +brassinolide >6x). Carvalho et al. 2011 (Plant Methods 7:18) noted as a materials catalogue only. Maree et al. 2025 (Sensors 25:4321) summarised; its two public datasets recorded with DOIs and put ON HOLD by user decision, with a 3-use scope limit and an explicit no-parameter-transfer rule. Cultivar closed as unrecoverable: market name only, variety_cultivar is the literal string unknown and a guessed cultivar is forbidden. Capture pack updated: 2_plant.csv variety -> 11 columns (market name, cultivar=unknown, vendor, lot, label photo, growth_habit, terminal_inflorescence, leaves_between_inflor, leaf_color, leaf_rugosity, bse_fuchsin_result); SCHEMA.md gained the enumerations, rules 7-8 and a "Why the phenotype columns exist" section naming the source per column; PROTOCOL.md gained the non-destructive fields at capture time and three genotype-proxy observations in the destructive session (sympodial termination and leaf counts, colour/rugosity on detached blades, optional 1% fuchsin acid at 0.027 MPa for 2 min -> dry patches = heterobaric). Addendum E REMOVED from the 4-plant session per user decision: shot later, separate session, on a plant that will not be destroyed, after G.2 settles the baseline; two carry-over notes kept in section 3 so E stays cheap. Acceptance rule declared 2026-10-07 NOT changed and now passes: library (13 photos with both scales) median signed disagreement +3.89 % -> +0.22 %, median abs 3.89 % -> 0.89 %, p10-p90 -1.32..+0.91 % -> width scale ADOPTED on the library set. 2026-10-08 | Claude | Prior-art note written after reading two papers from the professor: Butturini et al., in silico Plants 2026, 8, diaf024 (first FSP model for dwarf tomato in vertical farming, GroIMP + MTG + Monte-Carlo spectral ray tracing, cv. Heartbreakers F1 Twiggy Red, parameterized at 35/65 plants m-2 and validated on a separate experiment at 50/80, architecture from Photoneo structured-light scans of 6 DEFOLIATED plants merged into a probabilistic average MTG per density) and Usenko et al. (TLA from Meshroom SfM + XGBoost on 73 dwarf tomato plants, 418 measurements by sequential "onion" defoliation, R2 0.80 MAE 489 cm2 within experiment falling to R2 0.56 MAE 579 cm2 across experiments). Four points carried into the project record: (1) the published model predicts the REPRESENTATIVE plant at a density from an averaged MTG built on defoliated plants with a structured-light scanner, so this project's per-individual claim from 2 ordinary photographs of an intact plant is distinct - but only if the per-individual result is demonstrated, and C6 is 0.328 against a 0.59 gate; (2) their leaflet-curvature result reverses sign between static and dynamic light calculation (curl up +1.0/+2.8 % static, -1.4/-1.7 % dynamic), so a roll/bend setting validated against a static light calculation can have the wrong sign for growth - a direct caution for the deferred leaflet_roll_deg cell; contour shape is worth up to +5.6 % but only dynamically at high density, which supports the Addendum G contour work and makes its payoff density-dependent; (3) they exclude interjected leaflets "for simplicity" and model only primary leaflets, so the F.6/F.7/F.12 work here is an increment over the published model, and fertilizer ratio/concentration is absent from their model, leaving step 3 open ground; (4) sequential defoliation is the published method, used independently by both groups, so the plant-1 protocol is standard practice and citable, and Usenko's cross-experiment drop 0.80 -> 0.56 is the honest expectation for generalization that the conference text should state rather than quoting a single within-experiment number. Importable and proposed, not adopted: SLA linear in planting density (their eq. 3, consistent with Barbosa 2019), the Coussement et al. 2018 geometric leaflet model, and their published parameter tables for the carbon module. No code, contract or configuration change. | handoff/PRIOR_ART_DWARF_TOMATO_FSP_2026-10-08.md
2026-10-08 | Claude | Point clouds in C:\Users\sho36\Downloads\Tomato_data dropped from scope by user decision (2026-10-08); build/tomato_data_probe_20261008_01/ kept as a dead-end record only, nothing depends on it. Prior-art note deepened on the two papers instead. diaf024 additions: full published parameter set (phyllotaxy 137 deg, branch 30 deg, leaf inclination 70 deg SINGLE value for all densities, Tbase 5.7 C, 10 main / 4 side phytomers, leaflet LW 1 at rank 1 and 2 at rank >=2, internode 203 mg, leaf 111/547 mg, truss 488 mg, fruit delay 42.4 Cd), model structure (quadratic phytomer number on the main stem, linear on branches, beta growth function, Thornley 1998 photosynthesis with Vanthoor 2011 buffer feedback softened from binary to a gradual ramp, Q10 maintenance respiration with a (1-exp(-f*RGR5)) metabolic-activity factor, 21.5 M rays depth 10; roots a carbon sink only with no water or nutrient uptake; no flower/fruit abortion), and their validation table (LAI NRMSE 8.5/7.4 % excellent; fruit DM 15.7/17.4/19.0 % good but 32.3 % POOR at 80 plants m-2, PBIAS systematically negative and worsening with density). Their own diagnosis quoted: the single fixed leaf/leaflet elevation angle for all densities, absent internode elongation under crowding, and leaf curliness which they call hard to model AND to measure. Recorded as the opening for this project: all three are measurements of intact individual plants under different spacings, which is what the photo-based pipeline is an instrument for - a sharper claim than building a rival FSP model. Also recorded, from their scenario table: leaflet length:width ratio is worth under +-1 % of assimilation while contour shape reaches +5.6 % and curvature -4.9 %, so aspect is near-worthless for the carbon model even though F.11a bought +0.031 AP50 on the detector gate - a gate metric must not set the modelling priority. Usenko additions: scale from a known 15 cm pot diameter; feature selection collapsing to height, width and mesh surface area only; MAE 489 cm2 = 31 % of the 1573 cm2 mean; linear models within 0.1 R2 of XGBoost. New critique recorded: their headline R2 0.80 comes from a random 70/30 split of 418 measurements taken from 73 plants (~5.7 onion stages per plant), so stages of the same plant sit in both train and test - the independent unit is the plant, and their own cross-experiment R2 of 0.52-0.56 is consistent with that leak; the t-SNE they use to justify pooling cannot establish transfer. Same error class Addendum I.1 corrected here, so any accuracy this project reports must come from a leave-plant-out split. No code, contract or configuration change. | handoff/PRIOR_ART_DWARF_TOMATO_FSP_2026-10-08.md
2026-10-08 | Claude | Third paper read and added to the prior-art note: Zhuo & You, PlantMDE, Comput Electron Agric 239 (2025) 110925 - single-image monocular depth estimation for plant phenotyping, with an organ-wise auxiliary loss and the PlantDepth benchmark (32751 samples, 56 species, 8 sources, much of it rendered from 3D plant models with SAM pseudo-segmentation); as a feature extractor it cuts downstream RMSE by 10.2 % (Setaria biomass) to 29.5 % (tomato height) and raises stress classification accuracy by 12.1-19.2 %. Most important for this project is their training-mix ablation: Crops3D was the one source that made generalization WORSE, costing -10.27 % and -28.65 % on PlantStereo, because its RGB-D came from high-sparse point clouds giving incoherent structures and noisy textures - i.e. low-fidelity synthetic plant data actively degraded transfer to real data rather than merely failing to help. Recorded as external quantitative support for the C1-C7 realism gate and for not running Stage 1 at C6 0.328. Three weaknesses noted where this project is strong: PlantMDE gives zero-shot RELATIVE depth and needs per-domain fine-tuning with metric ground truth for metric depth (our board plus ruler give scale directly, which is what the I.4/I.4b work secures); it cannot recover occluded structure and its authors name plant skeleton priors from 3D scans as the unbuilt fix, which is what the teacher rig produces; and it is optimised for near-top-down views and degrades on horizon views, while our product is 1 top + 1 side. Recorded as an open option to use as a pinned frozen component of twoview inference, NOT adopted - CONVENTIONS forbids new dependencies and a pretrained backbone needs the same provenance treatment as the sha256-pinned detector; to be decided after G.2, not during. Added a table dividing the ground between the three papers, with the conclusion that their named gaps are one gap: nobody has metric, per-organ, per-individual structure for an intact plant under different spacings. | handoff/PRIOR_ART_DWARF_TOMATO_FSP_2026-10-08.md

2026-10-08 | Claude | Fourth paper added to the prior-art note: Liu et al., LeafTrackNet, Comput Electron Agric 256 (2027) 112417 - leaf identity tracking over time from daily top-down RGB (CanolaTrack: 5704 images, 31840 boxes, 184 plants, 31 days, dt 24 h, pot rotation; YOLOv10 + MobileNetV3 triplet embedding where the positive pair is the same leaf at different time points, memory bank + cosine + Hungarian; HOTA 87.56 vs 67.7-76.5 baselines, ID switches 4 vs 16-278). Recorded as prior art for step 5 (per-leaf comparison across capture dates - data/tracks/ exists, nothing built). Design lesson carried: geometric association (IoU/Kalman/centroid) breaks on growing plants, growth-invariant appearance embeddings plus temporal memory survived at dt 24 h. Differences recorded: our board gives measured pose so matching can run in the plant frame with appearance only for ambiguous cases (hybrid, not their appearance-only design); our unit is the compound leaf, leaflets inherit identity from station_index (leaflets within a compound are an embedding's worst case but rigidly co-located); our interval is days not 24 h and occlusion heavier, so their HOTA is an upper bound. Also noted: plant 1's sequential-defoliation series is a free identity-labelled sequence under our imaging conditions (fixed camera, known identities, ~8 steps) that can reject a matcher for occlusion-change robustness before any multi-date annotation is spent - same-day only, no growth drift. Nothing to build now; becomes relevant at the first repeated-capture series, after the capture pack and G.2. | handoff/PRIOR_ART_DWARF_TOMATO_FSP_2026-10-08.md

2026-10-13 | Claude | USER STATED: the lamp height 30 cm above the board is measured to the EMITTING SURFACE, not the housing - recorded in data/records/env_20261013/shelf_geometry_20261013.json (lamp.height_reference), which resolves height_is_emitting_surface=null in build/p2_lamp_calibration_20261013_01/readiness/readiness.json. Also recorded the P2 first-pass outcome in the definitions doc: the synthetic check showed wall reflectance is NOT IDENTIFIABLE from this measurement design - truth 0.4, fit selected 0.8 at the upper bound with flux scale 1.177 vs truth 1.2, while calibration RMS was 1.11% and the held-out point 1.86%. Reflectance and lamp flux trade off, so a good RMS is not evidence that the parameters are right, and more readings of the same kind will not fix it. The fix is a measurement, not a method: cover the white walls with a matte dark material and re-measure about ten of the same grid points; (uncovered - covered) is the reflected component measured directly, which removes reflectance from the free parameters. Covering is a low-reflectance bound, not a true zero, and must be labelled that way. | data/records/env_20261013/shelf_geometry_20261013.json, docs/정의집_토마토_2026-10-11.md

2026-10-13 | Claude | USER STATED: each LED bar's emitting length is about 50 cm (board is 60 cm wide); width still unavailable. Recorded in data/records/env_20261013/shelf_geometry_20261013.json. Refitting my analytic check with L=50 fixed made things WORSE and clearer: lamp-only RMS rose to 22.4% (vs 16.5% with L free), and lamp+uniform-diffuse reached 5.8% only by placing the bars at y0 = -30 cm, i.e. 30 cm OUTSIDE the board, which is physically impossible; the fitted diffuse term was 118 umol = 65% of the mean. Worse, that rim-fitted solution predicts the canopy plane at 163.6 vs measured 295.9 - a 45% miss WITH THE WRONG SIGN (it claims moving 8 cm up toward the lamp gets darker), which is the direct consequence of the unphysical placement. This agrees independently with Codex's synthetic result (reflectance not identifiable: truth 0.4, fit chose the 0.8 bound, RMS still 1.1%). Both routes have the same cause: reflected light is ~60-65% of the field and has never been measured, so the fit spends it on a constant and puts the lamp anywhere. Conclusion recorded: stop fitting reflectance; the wall-covering measurement (cover the white walls with matte dark material, re-measure ~10 of the same grid points, uncovered minus covered = the reflected component) is now a prerequisite, not an option. | data/records/env_20261013/shelf_geometry_20261013.json

2026-10-13 | Claude | USER MEASURED (caliper): pot inner top edge across flats 59 mm (outer 60 mm already recorded), medium surface 10 mm below the rim, walls straight with no taper. Cross-check passed: the session's own photometry put the outer rim 0.5-1.2 mm outside the soil boundary and 60-59 = 0.5 mm per side, so caliper and image agree at the low end. DECISION recorded: the inner line (soil boundary) becomes the primary scale reference because it is the sharpest line in both views and now has a measured length, with the outer 60 mm kept as cross-check. CRITICAL consequence recorded: that line is NOT in the rim plane - with straight walls it is a 59 mm square at a plane 10 mm BELOW the rim, hence further from the camera, so a scale solved there is larger than at the rim plane. At the measured camera distances 193-278 mm, treating the inner line as if it sat in the rim plane makes every real length 3.6-5.2% too large and areas 7-11% too large, which is four times the 1.1% the session was weighing and would enter all 444 leaflet / 67 compound mm values in build/topview_20261012_02. The reference must therefore travel as the pair (59 mm, plane = rim - 10 mm). plane_bias grows accordingly: at d ~ 230 mm a leaf 20 mm above the rim goes from about +9.5%/+20% to +14%/+31%, sign unchanged, still no correction factor because leaf height is unmeasured. 2026-10-13 | Claude | USER DECISION: skip the wall-covering measurement and stop fitting wall reflectance. Evidence recorded for why fitting cannot work with the data that exists: (a) Codex's synthetic check recovered reflectance 0.8 at the upper bound against a truth of 0.4 while calibration RMS was 1.11% and the held-out point 1.86%, so a good RMS said nothing about the parameter; (b) my analytic check with the lamp FIXED at its real geometry (3 bars, 50 cm emitting length, 5 cm apart, 30 cm to the emitting surface) and only [flux, diffuse] free gave rim RMS 9.2-19.3% and underpredicted the canopy plane by 19.7-57.7% in every lamp-position variant. Structural reason: about 60% of the light arrives after reflection from a white ceiling beside the lamp, so the reflected part grows with height and a height-independent diffuse constant cannot represent it. New prompt handoff/CODEX_CLI_PROMPT_P2_EMPIRICAL_LIGHT_FIELD_2026-10-13.md: interpolate the 46 measured points as the incident-irradiance boundary (empirical_light_field), refuse extrapolation above 15 cm and outside the tray block, carry the unmeasured direction distribution as a declared scenario set (diffuse 0.5/0.6/0.7) rather than a fitted truth, let the engine compute canopy self-shading only, bind the field to a configuration hash so any geometry change invalidates it mechanically, and feed reference_dli through the consumer already built. Accepted cost recorded in the definitions doc: 'what if the lamp moves' is no longer answerable by prediction and becomes a re-measurement experiment. 2026-10-13 | Claude | F9 재확인(요청). 보고서 자체 표만으로 전부 재계산해 내부 일관성 확인: 임계값 = 수요 0.1536 − 총광합성 0.0118 + 유지호흡 0.0002 = 0.1420 gC로 보고된 0.14203과 일치; 충족분율도 단일 식 (예비+총광합성−유지호흡)/수요 로 0.05→0.4010, 0.10→0.7266, 0.12→0.8568, 0.14→0.9870, 0.1415→0.9967 로 보고값과 소수 4자리까지 재현. 따라서 9개 스윕 실행은 서로 독립인 9개 사실이 아니라 하나의 해석식을 확인한 것(4.8M ray × 9회 비용 주의, 오류는 아님). NEW FINDING (보고서에 없는 것): high(2.0) 시나리오는 포화 상태다. base 표에서 S/M = 0.03935/0.07218 = 0.5452, 총수요 = 0.11153/0.7263 = 0.15356 gC → 주지 수요 Dm = 0.09938, 곁순 수요 Ds = 0.05418 gC. high의 S 배분 0.05417 gC는 Ds와 반올림 내에서 같다 — 즉 곁순이 자기 수요 전부를 받아 상한에 걸려 있고, 잉여는 주지로 돌아갔다. 포화 척도는 k* = Dm/(F−Ds) = 0.09938/(0.11153−0.05418) = 1.733이므로 SUBSTEM_SINK_SCALE이 약 1.73 이상이면 어떤 값이든 같은 결과가 나오고, 선언된 상한 2.0은 절단(censored)되어 실제로 적용된 유효 척도는 1.73이다. 결과적으로 '방향은 선언대로 움직인다'는 검사는 통과하지만 low_high_distance의 상단은 선언값이 아니라 포화점이 정하며, 2.0을 5.0으로 올려도 숫자는 달라지지 않는다. 코드 쪽 확인 요청: 가중 배분에 min(가중지분, 수요)에 해당하는 절단이 실제로 있는지, 있으면 포화 여부를 manifest에 기록할 것(시나리오 집합의 상단이 선언값으로 해석되면 불확실성 폭을 잘못 읽게 된다).
